MOV 0x00 data-movement 2 forms 24 mnemonics public evidence notes

Moves data into a register: MOV copies a source register, MVN copies its bitwise inverse (the N modifier), LOAD_IMM loads an 11-bit signed immediate, and LOAD_SIMM additionally shifts that immediate left by 0-31 bit positions before loading - the idiom for building constants and masks without a memory access. MOVS (S) is the canonical way to test a register value. Writing PC as the destination branches - ordinary unless S is set, which schedules the branch (and sets no flags). S sets the flags but never the carry: C retains its previous value. To test a value without storing a result (the CMP/TST idiom), use GPI as the discard destination.

Restrictions: No support for SPO/SNE condition flags; S with destination PC does not set flags (schedules branch instead)

LDR 0x01 memory 1 forms 24 mnemonics public evidence notes

Reads memory over the AHB data bus into a register, addressed by a pointer register plus an 8-bit signed immediate offset - word-scaled for word reads, byte-scaled for byte reads. B reads a byte (zero-extended), BS a byte with sign extension. PRE/POST variants write the offset pointer update back to the pointer register before or after the access. The read is submitted and execution continues: the DFETCH unit lands the data in the background, so a load costs one cycle unless the destination register is consumed before the data returns. The POP aliases are presets of this form - the stack grows upward, so POP pre-decrements SP by one word.

Restrictions: Post-increment of PC is not allowed; A pointer register that is also the read destination gets no address update - PRE or POST, the update is ignored; writeback is also ignored when the pointer is PC; No support for SPO/SNE condition flags; Reads using CFS as the pointer see the bottom 8 bits of CFS as 0x00; hardware behavior still needs final confirmation; CFS cannot be the destination of a byte read; S on byte loads means signed access (bit 21), not set-flags; loads never set flags; PUSH/POP: byte and word stacking must not be mixed across non-word-aligned boundaries - SP bits [1:0] are ignored in word-access address calculation (AN14650 5.29); Word accesses truncate the address to word alignment (bits [1:0] ignored)

default

STR 0x02 memory 1 forms 16 mnemonics public evidence notes

Writes a register to memory over the AHB data bus, addressed by a pointer register plus an 8-bit signed immediate offset (word-scaled for word stores, byte-scaled for byte stores); B stores the bottom byte. PRE/POST variants write the offset pointer update back before or after the access. Stores share E_LDR's submit-and-continue performance. The PUSH aliases are presets of this form - the stack grows upward, so PUSH post-increments SP by one word. Do not rely on duplicate byte-lane side effects for byte stores without hardware confirmation. Note the operand order: the address register comes FIRST (E_STR(addr, data, offset)) - the reverse of the load spelling's destination-first order, a classic swapped-store hazard.

Restrictions: Post-increment of PC is not allowed; AN14650 5.25 carries a pointer/post-update cancellation phrased for reads (a 5.24 copy-paste smell); for stores, treat the data value as latched before any pointer update; No support for SPO/SNE condition flags; Cannot set flags (no S variant); PUSH/POP: byte and word stacking must not be mixed across non-word-aligned boundaries - SP bits [1:0] are ignored in word-access address calculation (AN14650 5.29); Word accesses truncate the address to word alignment (bits [1:0] ignored)

default

Public Provenance

  • E_STR*/E_PUSH* encoder formulas (bunny_build__instr.h / .c encoders)

GOSUB 0x03 control 1 forms 1 mnemonics public evidence notes

Calls a subroutine at an absolute 30-bit word address - any word in the 4GB space: any instruction word whose bits [1:0] are 0b11 is a GOSUB, and the remaining 30 bits are the word address directly. RA is always overwritten with the return address - the word after the GOSUB - so nested calls must stack RA explicitly (the stack grows upward); return by branching through RA. Because GOSUB overlays the whole encoding space, a decoder must ensure that any word with bits [1:0] = 0b11 decodes as GOSUB. This is safe by construction: every other defined opcode value has [1:0] != 0b11, so no other pattern can match such a word - under most-specific-first ordering GOSUB simply falls out last, naturally and correctly.

Restrictions: Target address must be word aligned (bits [1:0] of the address are implied 0b00, replaced by the 0b11 tag); No conditional variant: the cond field bits belong to the target address; RA is always overwritten; preserve it manually for nesting; Cannot set flags (no S variant)

default

E_GOSUB (label)

fixed bits = decode mask/match; tinted cells = operand and modifier fields; dim 0 = reserved-zero canonical output
Mnemonic Base Word Notes
E_GOSUB 0x00000003 Calls the 30-bit word address; RA receives the return address
  • decode mask 0x00000003, match 0x00000003
  • Only bits [1:0] are fixed; bits [4:2] of the opcode field carry address bits.

Public Provenance

  • E_GOSUB label prototype; two-pass assembler emits 0x03 + target address (bunny_build__instr.h / .c encoders)
  • section 5.23: branch to immediate 30-bit word address, RA always updated for stacking/returning; Table 12: three cycles (AN14650 SmartDMA Cookbook Rev 1.0 (2025-05-28))
PER_READ 0x04 peripheral-access 1 forms 2 mnemonics public evidence notes

Reads a peripheral register into a destination register with pure immediate addressing: the immediate is a word-aligned byte offset from 0x4000_0000 (bits [1:0] forced to zero; 18 offset bits are encoded), reaching the 1MB peripheral window - no register needs to be spent on peripheral addressing. Performance matches E_LDR: submitted in one cycle, data lands via DFETCH. (Note: AN14650 5.28 says the offset "covers a 22-bit offset region" - arithmetically impossible for the encoded field; the hardware address computation, both headers, and real camera code agree on 1MB.) The macro argument is the offset itself, not the absolute peripheral address.

Restrictions: Address must be word aligned; No support for SPO/SNE condition flags; Cannot set flags (no S variant)

default

E_COND_PER_READ 0x00000004 Reads the peripheral at 0x4000_0000 + offset into DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_)
E_PER_READ 0x00000004 Reads the peripheral at 0x4000_0000 + offset into DST
  • decode mask 0x0000001f, match 0x00000004
  • Unlike PER_WRITE, the address field is contiguous: instr [31:14] <- addr [19:2].

Public Provenance

  • section 5.28: peripheral read, immediate offset from 0x40000000, performance same as E_LDR. Its 22-bit-region note contradicts the 18-bit encoded field (AN14650 defect #3, recorded in prose) (AN14650 SmartDMA Cookbook Rev 1.0 (2025-05-28))
PER_WRITE 0x05 peripheral-access 1 forms 2 mnemonics public evidence notes

Writes a source register to a peripheral register with pure immediate addressing: the immediate is a word-aligned byte offset from 0x4000_0000 (bits [1:0] forced to zero; 18 offset bits are encoded), reaching the 1MB peripheral window - no register needs to be spent on peripheral addressing. Performance matches E_LDR (AN14650 section 5.28). (Note: AN14650 5.28 says the offset "covers a 22-bit offset region" - arithmetically impossible for the encoded field; the hardware address computation, both headers, and real camera code agree on 1MB.) The macro argument is the offset itself, not the absolute peripheral address.

Restrictions: Address must be word aligned; No support for SPO/SNE condition flags; Cannot set flags (no S variant)

ADD 0x06 alu 2 forms 88 mnemonics public evidence notes

Adds two registers (dest = src1 + src2) or a register and a 12-bit signed immediate. The result then passes through the optional post-stages in mnemonic-spelling order: N inverts it, F flips its byte order, and a fused LSL/LSR/ASR/ROR shifts or rotates it by a 0-31 immediate; flags (S) update last. The IMM form uses a 12-bit signed immediate with N and S only (the immediate occupies the shift bits). Writing PC as the destination branches - an ordinary branch unless S is set, which makes it a scheduled branch (and sets no flags). SPO/SNE conditions are legal with this family - the algorithmic shift+ALU idiom (see the shift family for their full semantics). When they cancel the ALU stage, it passes through its LEFT operand: here that is src1, which then still goes through any N inversion and fused post-shift before writeback. src2 is discarded.

Restrictions: S with destination PC does not set flags (schedules branch instead); IMM form has no shift fusion (immediate occupies the shift bits)

reg

  • Mnemonics without a shift suffix are the LSL encoding with shift5 = 0.

imm

E_[COND_]ADD[N]_IMM[S] ([cond,] dest, src1, imm12s)

fixed bits = decode mask/match; tinted cells = operand and modifier fields; dim 0 = reserved-zero canonical output
Mnemonic Base Word Notes
E_ADDN_IMM 0x000c0006 Adds the 12-bit signed immediate to SRC1, result to DST; inverts the ALU result (N)
E_ADDN_IMMS 0x000c0206 Adds the 12-bit signed immediate to SRC1, result to DST; inverts the ALU result (N); sets the ALU flags (S)
E_ADD_IMM 0x00040006 Adds the 12-bit signed immediate to SRC1, result to DST
E_ADD_IMMS 0x00040206 Adds the 12-bit signed immediate to SRC1, result to DST; sets the ALU flags (S)
E_COND_ADDN_IMM 0x000c0006 Adds the 12-bit signed immediate to SRC1, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_); inverts the ALU result (N)
E_COND_ADDN_IMMS 0x000c0206 Adds the 12-bit signed immediate to SRC1, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_); inverts the ALU result (N); sets the ALU flags (S)
E_COND_ADD_IMM 0x00040006 Adds the 12-bit signed immediate to SRC1, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_)
E_COND_ADD_IMMS 0x00040206 Adds the 12-bit signed immediate to SRC1, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_); sets the ALU flags (S)
  • decode mask 0x0004001f, match 0x00040006

Public Provenance

  • E_ADD* encoder formulas (family matrix extracted by isa/tools/extract_header.py) (bunny_build__instr.h / .c encoders)
  • section 5.2: purpose, options (invertible data, result byte-order flip, postshift 0-31), imm12s, PC restriction; Table 12: single cycle (AN14650 SmartDMA Cookbook Rev 1.0 (2025-05-28))
SUB 0x08 alu 2 forms 88 mnemonics public evidence notes

Subtracts register from register (dest = src1 - src2) or a 12-bit signed immediate from a register. The result then passes through the optional post-stages in mnemonic-spelling order: N inverts it, F flips its byte order, and a fused LSL/LSR/ASR/ROR shifts or rotates it by a 0-31 immediate; flags (S) update last. The IMM form uses a 12-bit signed immediate with N and S only (the immediate occupies the shift bits). Writing PC as the destination branches - an ordinary branch unless S is set, which makes it a scheduled branch (and sets no flags). SPO/SNE conditions are legal with this family - the algorithmic shift+ALU idiom (see the shift family for their full semantics). When they cancel the ALU stage, it passes through its LEFT operand: here that is src1, which then still goes through any N inversion and fused post-shift before writeback. src2 is discarded.

Restrictions: S with destination PC does not set flags (schedules branch instead); IMM form has no shift fusion (immediate occupies the shift bits)

reg

E_[COND_]SUB[N]_[F]{LSL|ASR|LSR|ROR}[S] ([cond,] dest, src1, src2 [, shift5])

fixed bits = decode mask/match; tinted cells = operand and modifier fields; dim 0 = reserved-zero canonical output

imm

Public Provenance

  • E_SUB* encoder formulas (family matrix extracted by isa/tools/extract_header.py) (bunny_build__instr.h / .c encoders)
  • section 5.3: purpose, options (invertible data, result byte-order flip, postshift 0-31), imm12s, PC restriction; Table 12: single cycle (AN14650 SmartDMA Cookbook Rev 1.0 (2025-05-28))
ADC 0x09 alu 2 forms 88 mnemonics public evidence notes

Adds with carry (dest = src1 + src2 + C) - the building block for multi-word arithmetic. The result then passes through the optional post-stages in mnemonic-spelling order: N inverts it, F flips its byte order, and a fused LSL/LSR/ASR/ROR shifts or rotates it by a 0-31 immediate; flags (S) update last. The IMM form uses a 12-bit signed immediate with N and S only (the immediate occupies the shift bits). Writing PC as the destination branches - an ordinary branch unless S is set, which makes it a scheduled branch (and sets no flags). SPO/SNE conditions are legal with this family - the algorithmic shift+ALU idiom (see the shift family for their full semantics). When they cancel the ALU stage, it passes through its LEFT operand: here that is src1, which then still goes through any N inversion and fused post-shift before writeback. src2 is discarded.

Restrictions: S with destination PC does not set flags (schedules branch instead); IMM form has no shift fusion (immediate occupies the shift bits)

reg

E_[COND_]ADC[N]_[F]{LSL|ASR|LSR|ROR}[S] ([cond,] dest, src1, src2 [, shift5])

fixed bits = decode mask/match; tinted cells = operand and modifier fields; dim 0 = reserved-zero canonical output

  • section 5.4: purpose, options (invertible data, result byte-order flip, postshift 0-31), imm12s, PC restriction; Table 12: single cycle (AN14650 SmartDMA Cookbook Rev 1.0 (2025-05-28))
SBC 0x0A alu 2 forms 88 mnemonics public evidence notes

Subtracts, then subtracts the carry flag (dest = src1 - src2 - C) - the building block for multi-word arithmetic. The result then passes through the optional post-stages in mnemonic-spelling order: N inverts it, F flips its byte order, and a fused LSL/LSR/ASR/ROR shifts or rotates it by a 0-31 immediate; flags (S) update last. The IMM form uses a 12-bit signed immediate with N and S only (the immediate occupies the shift bits). Writing PC as the destination branches - an ordinary branch unless S is set, which makes it a scheduled branch (and sets no flags). SPO/SNE conditions are legal with this family - the algorithmic shift+ALU idiom (see the shift family for their full semantics). When they cancel the ALU stage, it passes through its LEFT operand: here that is src1, which then still goes through any N inversion and fused post-shift before writeback. src2 is discarded.

Restrictions: S with destination PC does not set flags (schedules branch instead); IMM form has no shift fusion (immediate occupies the shift bits)

reg

E_[COND_]SBC[N]_[F]{LSL|ASR|LSR|ROR}[S] ([cond,] dest, src1, src2 [, shift5])

fixed bits = decode mask/match; tinted cells = operand and modifier fields; dim 0 = reserved-zero canonical output

OR 0x0C alu 2 forms 88 mnemonics public evidence notes

Bitwise ORs (dest = src1 OR src2, or src1 OR imm12s) - the bit-set primitive. The result then passes through the optional post-stages in mnemonic-spelling order: N inverts it, F flips its byte order, and a fused LSL/LSR/ASR/ROR shifts or rotates it by a 0-31 immediate; flags (S) update last. The IMM form uses a 12-bit signed immediate with N and S only (the immediate occupies the shift bits). Writing PC as the destination branches - an ordinary branch unless S is set, which makes it a scheduled branch (and sets no flags). S here never changes the carry flag: only ADD/SUB/ADC/SBC and shifts can affect C, so C retains its previous value. SPO/SNE conditions are legal with this family - the algorithmic shift+ALU idiom (see the shift family for their full semantics). When they cancel the ALU stage, it passes through its LEFT operand: here that is src1, which then still goes through any N inversion and fused post-shift before writeback. src2 is discarded.

Restrictions: S with destination PC does not set flags (schedules branch instead); IMM form has no shift fusion (immediate occupies the shift bits)

reg

  • Mnemonics without a shift suffix are the LSL encoding with shift5 = 0.

imm

E_[COND_]OR[N]_IMM[S] ([cond,] dest, src1, imm12s)

fixed bits = decode mask/match; tinted cells = operand and modifier fields; dim 0 = reserved-zero canonical output
Mnemonic Base Word Notes
E_COND_ORN_IMM 0x000c000c ORs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_); inverts the ALU result (N)
E_COND_ORN_IMMS 0x000c020c ORs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_); inverts the ALU result (N); sets the ALU flags (S)
E_COND_OR_IMM 0x0004000c ORs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_)
E_COND_OR_IMMS 0x0004020c ORs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_); sets the ALU flags (S)
E_ORN_IMM 0x000c000c ORs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST; inverts the ALU result (N)
E_ORN_IMMS 0x000c020c ORs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST; inverts the ALU result (N); sets the ALU flags (S)
E_OR_IMM 0x0004000c ORs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST
E_OR_IMMS 0x0004020c ORs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST; sets the ALU flags (S)
  • decode mask 0x0004001f, match 0x0004000c

Public Provenance

  • E_OR* encoder formulas (family matrix extracted by isa/tools/extract_header.py) (bunny_build__instr.h / .c encoders)
  • section 5.7: purpose, options (invertible data, result byte-order flip, postshift 0-31), imm12s, PC restriction; Table 12: single cycle (AN14650 SmartDMA Cookbook Rev 1.0 (2025-05-28))
AND 0x0D alu 2 forms 88 mnemonics public evidence notes

Bitwise ANDs (dest = src1 AND src2, or src1 AND imm12s) - the mask primitive. The result then passes through the optional post-stages in mnemonic-spelling order: N inverts it, F flips its byte order, and a fused LSL/LSR/ASR/ROR shifts or rotates it by a 0-31 immediate; flags (S) update last. The IMM form uses a 12-bit signed immediate with N and S only (the immediate occupies the shift bits). Writing PC as the destination branches - an ordinary branch unless S is set, which makes it a scheduled branch (and sets no flags). S here never changes the carry flag: only ADD/SUB/ADC/SBC and shifts can affect C, so C retains its previous value. SPO/SNE conditions are legal with this family - the algorithmic shift+ALU idiom (see the shift family for their full semantics). When they cancel the ALU stage, it passes through its LEFT operand: here that is src1, which then still goes through any N inversion and fused post-shift before writeback. src2 is discarded.

Restrictions: S with destination PC does not set flags (schedules branch instead); IMM form has no shift fusion (immediate occupies the shift bits)

reg

E_[COND_]AND[N]_[F]{LSL|ASR|LSR|ROR}[S] ([cond,] dest, src1, src2 [, shift5])

fixed bits = decode mask/match; tinted cells = operand and modifier fields; dim 0 = reserved-zero canonical output

imm

E_[COND_]AND[N]_IMM[S] ([cond,] dest, src1, imm12s)

fixed bits = decode mask/match; tinted cells = operand and modifier fields; dim 0 = reserved-zero canonical output
Mnemonic Base Word Notes
E_ANDN_IMM 0x000c000d ANDs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST; inverts the ALU result (N)
E_ANDN_IMMS 0x000c020d ANDs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST; inverts the ALU result (N); sets the ALU flags (S)
E_AND_IMM 0x0004000d ANDs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST
E_AND_IMMS 0x0004020d ANDs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST; sets the ALU flags (S)
E_COND_ANDN_IMM 0x000c000d ANDs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_); inverts the ALU result (N)
E_COND_ANDN_IMMS 0x000c020d ANDs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_); inverts the ALU result (N); sets the ALU flags (S)
E_COND_AND_IMM 0x0004000d ANDs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_)
E_COND_AND_IMMS 0x0004020d ANDs SRC1 with the 12-bit signed immediate, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_); sets the ALU flags (S)
  • decode mask 0x0004001f, match 0x0004000d

Public Provenance

  • E_AND* encoder formulas (family matrix extracted by isa/tools/extract_header.py) (bunny_build__instr.h / .c encoders)
  • section 5.6: purpose, options (invertible data, result byte-order flip, postshift 0-31), imm12s, PC restriction; Table 12: single cycle (AN14650 SmartDMA Cookbook Rev 1.0 (2025-05-28))
XOR 0x0E alu 2 forms 88 mnemonics public evidence notes

Bitwise XORs (dest = src1 XOR src2, or src1 XOR imm12s) - the bit-toggle primitive. The result then passes through the optional post-stages in mnemonic-spelling order: N inverts it, F flips its byte order, and a fused LSL/LSR/ASR/ROR shifts or rotates it by a 0-31 immediate; flags (S) update last. The IMM form uses a 12-bit signed immediate with N and S only (the immediate occupies the shift bits). Writing PC as the destination branches - an ordinary branch unless S is set, which makes it a scheduled branch (and sets no flags). S here never changes the carry flag: only ADD/SUB/ADC/SBC and shifts can affect C, so C retains its previous value. SPO/SNE conditions are legal with this family - the algorithmic shift+ALU idiom (see the shift family for their full semantics). When they cancel the ALU stage, it passes through its LEFT operand: here that is src1, which then still goes through any N inversion and fused post-shift before writeback. src2 is discarded.

Restrictions: S with destination PC does not set flags (schedules branch instead); IMM form has no shift fusion (immediate occupies the shift bits)

reg

E_[COND_]XOR[N]_[F]{LSL|ASR|LSR|ROR}[S] ([cond,] dest, src1, src2 [, shift5])

fixed bits = decode mask/match; tinted cells = operand and modifier fields; dim 0 = reserved-zero canonical output

  • section 5.8: purpose, options (invertible data, result byte-order flip, postshift 0-31), imm12s, PC restriction; Table 12: single cycle (AN14650 SmartDMA Cookbook Rev 1.0 (2025-05-28))
SHIFT 0x10 shift 1 forms 128 mnemonics public evidence notes

Pre-shifts or pre-rotates a register, then optionally feeds the shifted value through an ALU operation: dest = (R2shift shifted by a 0-31 immediate) OP Roperand, with OP one of ADD/ADC/SUB/SBC/AND/OR/XOR - the shifted value is the LEFT operand, which matters for SUB/SBC - or a plain shift when no operation is fused. The mnemonic spells execution order: E_LSL_ADD shifts first, then adds. Together with the SPO/SNE conditions this family implements the algorithmic shift-and-accumulate idiom. The algorithmic conditions in full: SPO/SNE cancel only the fused ALU stage - the shift and the writeback still execute, and an N inversion is NOT cancelled; the algorithmic flags themselves latch only from an opcode that was conditioned on SPO/SNE. This is the binary-multiply idiom: shift every iteration, operate only when the MSB says so. When the ALU stage is cancelled it passes through its LEFT operand; every other pipeline stage still runs. In this family the left operand is the shifted value, so the result is (src2 shifted) with src1 discarded - which is what makes shift-and-accumulate work. In the post-shift ALU families (ADD/SUB/ADC/SBC/AND/OR/XOR with a fused shift) the left operand is src1 instead, so the result is src1 put through the post-shift and any N inversion. Same rule, different operand, because the families order the shifter and the ALU differently. For fused ADC/SBC with a nonzero shift amount, treat the carry-in as coming from the shift stage rather than the CA flag unless a bench test says otherwise.

Restrictions: S with destination PC does not set flags (schedules branch instead)

default

E_[COND_]{LSL|ASR|LSR|ROR}[_{AND|OR|XOR|ADD|SUB|ADC|SBC}][S] ([cond,] dest, [roperand,] r2shift, shift5)

fixed bits = decode mask/match; tinted cells = operand and modifier fields; dim 0 = reserved-zero canonical output

  • decode mask 0x0000001f, match 0x00000010
  • src1 [17:14] is the ALU operand register, present only when fuse_op != 0. src2 [23:20] is the register being shifted.

Public Provenance

  • E_LSL*/E_LSR*/E_ASR*/E_ROR* encoder formulas (bunny_build__instr.h / .c encoders)
  • section 5.10: preshift + ALU operation, shift by imm 0-31; Table 12: single cycle (AN14650 SmartDMA Cookbook Rev 1.0 (2025-05-28))
FSHIFT 0x11 shift 1 forms 128 mnemonics public evidence notes

Flips the operand's byte order (FEND) or bit order (FBIT), right-shifts the flipped value (LSR or ASR by a 0-31 immediate), then optionally applies a fused ALU operation with the flipped-and-shifted value as the LEFT operand (dest = shifted OP Roperand - the order matters for SUB/SBC). Bit-order flip converts LSB-first serial data to MSB-first and back; byte-order flip converts endianness - with the shift and ALU stage this normalizes protocol data in one cycle. SPO/SNE cancel only the fused ALU stage here (see the shift family for the full algorithmic-flags story).

Restrictions: S with destination PC does not set flags (schedules branch instead); Only right shifts (LSR/ASR) exist in this family

default

E_[COND_]{FEND|FBIT}_{LSR|ASR}[_{AND|OR|XOR|ADD|SUB|ADC|SBC}][S] ([cond,] dest, [roperand,] r2shift, shift5)

fixed bits = decode mask/match; tinted cells = operand and modifier fields; dim 0 = reserved-zero canonical output
Mnemonic Base Word Notes
E_COND_FBIT_ASR 0x000c0011 Flips R2shift, then right-shifts it by Shift5imm, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_); bit-order flip (FBIT); arithmetic right shift (ASR)
E_COND_FBIT_ASRS 0x000c0211 Flips R2shift, then right-shifts it by Shift5imm, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_); bit-order flip (FBIT); arithmetic right shift (ASR); sets the ALU flags (S)
E_COND_FBIT_ASR_ADC 0xc00c0011 Flips R2shift, then right-shifts it by Shift5imm, result to DST; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_); bit-order flip (FBIT); arithmetic right shift (ASR); fused ADC of Roperand

NOP 0x12 control 5 forms 5 mnemonics public evidence notes

Opcode 0x12 is a small control family selected by a sub-op field. NOP does nothing for one cycle. HEART_RYTHM loads the 16-bit heartbeat counter (from an immediate or a register) - the timebase for deterministic-interval execution. SYNCH_ALL_TO_BEAT switches the synchronize-all-instructions-to-beat feature on or off with a 1-bit immediate. WAIT_FOR_BEAT halts until the next heartbeat, the idiom for aligning work (serial sampling, for example) to fixed intervals; load the counter with HEART_RYTHM first. (In the original header E_NOP is an object-like macro, written without parentheses.)

Restrictions: No conditional variants: bits [8:5] are the sub-op selector, not a cond field; Cannot set flags (no S variants)

INT_TRIGGER 0x14 control 1 forms 1 mnemonics public evidence notes

Fires a single-cycle pulse on the EZH interrupt outputs: the 24-bit immediate mask selects which of the 24 individual interrupt channels pulse, and the common interrupt output always fires regardless of the mask - the mechanism for signalling the host CPU (frame complete, buffer ready) without touching a bus.

Restrictions: The common interrupt output triggers regardless of the channel mask - so a zero mask (E_INT_TRIGGER(0)) still raises the common interrupt and is the idiomatic end-of-program signal; No conditional variant: the 24-bit mask occupies the cond field bits; Cannot set flags (no S variant)

GOTO 0x15 control 2 forms 8 mnemonics public evidence notes

Branches to a word-aligned target: a 21-bit word immediate (label form, bit 9 set) reaching the current 8MB window - the top 9 address bits are preserved from PC - or a full 32-bit address from a register (bit 9 clear). The L variants back up a return address to RA: after a scheduled branch RA = the GOTO's address + 12 (the word after the two unflushed slots); after an ordinary branch RA = the GOTO's address + 4 (the next word). SPO/UNS conditions always schedule the branch (the next two opcodes still execute); SNE/NZS schedule it when NZ is set. An ordinary branch flushes the two prefetched opcodes and costs three cycles; a scheduled branch costs one.

Restrictions: Immediate target must be word aligned (encoding relies on address bits [1:0] being zero); Immediate target reaches the current 8MB window: the 21-bit word immediate fills target bits [22:2] and the top 9 address bits are preserved from PC (AN14650 5.22; the hardware computes (PC & 0xff800000) | (imm << 2)). bunny_build masks the byte address with 0x1FFFFF and populates only instr [29:11] - the low 2MB of the window; widening it is a recorded assembler improvement opportunity; Cannot set flags (no S variant)

ANDOR 0x16 alu 1 forms 4 mnemonics public evidence notes

dest = (rsrc AND rand) OR ror: masks a source register with an AND operand, then ORs in a third register - simultaneously clearing and setting specific bits in a single instruction (AN14650 section 5.9). No shift or rotate is involved: the historical argument name "ror" reads register-to-OR, not rotate-right. Corrected 2026-07-21 - this file previously typed the fourth operand as a 5-bit rotate amount, an interpretation contradicted by both the hardware reference model ((src1 & src2) | src3) and AN14650's option list, which names only conditional execution and set-flag.

Restrictions: Encoding remains marked for hardware confirmation; No support for SPO/SNE condition flags; S with destination PC does not set flags (schedules branch instead); OR-source register limited to R0-R7 in the current model; field width still needs hardware confirmation

BITOP 0x18 alu 2 forms 32 mnemonics public evidence notes

Tests or modifies a single bit of Rdata, selected by a register or a 5-bit immediate: BSET sets it, BCLR clears it, BTOG toggles it, and BTST isolates it - the destination receives Rdata AND (1 << bit), so unlike ARM's TST the destination IS written (use GPI as the discard destination). Pair BTST with S to set flags on the tested bit. The register-select form lets a loop walk a bit index. S never changes the carry flag here (C retains its previous value).

Restrictions: No support for SPO/SNE condition flags; S with destination PC does not set flags (schedules branch instead)

RSHIFT 0x19 shift 1 forms 480 mnemonics public evidence notes

Shifts by a register-supplied amount: the bottom byte of the shift register (0-255) drives an RLSL/RLSR/RASR/RROR of R2shift, optionally fused with an ALU operation against Roperand in either order - shift-then-operate (E_RLSL_ADD) or operate-then-shift (E_ADD_RLSL); the mnemonic spells the execution order, and N inverts the ALU result. This is the variable-shift workhorse for bit-packing loops whose field widths are computed at run time. (Note: AN14650 5.12's description sentence says "byte or bit order flipping" - a copy-paste from 5.11; its own options list and the encoding model agree this family shifts by the lower 8 bits of a register.) Amounts of 32 and above still need bench confirmation. The model used here rotates amounts modulo 32 and saturates shifts - the value shifts fully out, with the carry capturing the last bit at exactly 32 and clearing beyond 32. With SPO/SNE and an LSL by 1, the carry is automatically shifted into the LSB - the tailchaining idiom for chaining shift work across registers. SPO/SNE cancel only the fused ALU stage here (see the shift family for the full algorithmic-flags story). This family is the one that carries BOTH geometries, selected by the order bit, so the surviving operand differs within it: cancellation passes through the ALU's LEFT operand, which for shift-then-operate (E_RLSL_ADD) is the shifted src2, and for operate-then-shift (E_ADD_RLSL) is src1, which then still goes through the shift stage. Same rule as the immediate-shift and post-shift families - applied twice, because both orders live here. For fused ADC/SBC with a nonzero shift amount, treat the carry-in as coming from the shift stage rather than the CA flag unless a bench test says otherwise.

Restrictions: S with destination PC does not set flags (schedules branch instead); Shift-amount register is a 3-bit field: R0-R7 only (bit 27 is the N modifier); Shift amounts >= 32 follow the model described above, but still need bench confirmation

default

TIGHT_LOOP 0x1A control 1 forms 2 mnemonics public evidence notes

Configures the Execution Supervisor for zero-overhead looping: Rend points one word past the loop body's final opcode, and Rcount gives how many times the block repeats after its first pass - the body runs Rcount + 1 times in total. The instruction immediately following E_TIGHT_LOOP executes once only; the instruction after that is the first instruction of the repeated block. Iteration tracking and the branch back cost no instruction cycles.

Restrictions: The instruction immediately following E_TIGHT_LOOP executes only once; the repeated block starts at the instruction after it; To repeat a single instruction, set Rend to 3 words past the E_TIGHT_LOOP address; Cannot set flags (no S variant); Corner cases remain open: a zero-iteration loop is inexpressible (Rcount = 0 still runs the body once), and whether Rend/Rcount are snapshotted at E_TIGHT_LOOP execution or sampled live is unstated

default

E_[COND_]TIGHT_LOOP ([cond,] rend, rcount)

fixed bits = decode mask/match; tinted cells = operand and modifier fields; dim 0 = reserved-zero canonical output
Mnemonic Base Word Notes
E_COND_TIGHT_LOOP 0x0000001a Arms the zero-overhead loop: Rend = end address, Rcount = extra repeats; executes only when the Flag condition holds (COND_)
E_TIGHT_LOOP 0x0000001a Arms the zero-overhead loop: Rend = end address, Rcount = extra repeats
  • decode mask 0x0000001f, match 0x0000001a
  • End-address register rides in src1 [17:14], loop count register in src2 [23:20].

Public Provenance

  • E_TIGHT_LOOP(rend,rcount) = 0x1A + (rend<<14) + (rcount<<20) (bunny_build__instr.h / .c encoders)
  • section 5.15: execution supervisor, Rend/Rcount semantics (runs count+1 times), single-instruction-loop rule; Table 12: single cycle (AN14650 SmartDMA Cookbook Rev 1.0 (2025-05-28))
HOLD 0x1C control 3 forms 18 mnemonics public evidence notes

Halts execution until the Boolean pattern detector fires. Plain HOLD parks at the opcode until the bit-slice detector (configured through CFS/CFM) evaluates true, then falls through. VECTORED_HOLD monitors several ORed conditions at once and dispatches through a vector table when any fires: slice 0 has the highest priority, and an ANDed slice group dispatches on its last slice. The first table entry lives at the table register value + 4 (PC works as the base when the table follows two unflushed opcodes); entries are one word each, or four words with LV (large vector) - room for a scheduled branch, its two unflushed opcodes, and one more instruction. By default the resume address is backed up to RA so the handler can return; NRA suppresses that. ACC variants carry a per-vector acceleration mask in [31:24] (bit 24+n accelerates vector n): for an accelerated vector the two already-prefetched opcodes are not flushed, so handler work starts during the dispatch. The minimum monitor-to-handler-to-monitor round trip is 4 cycles.

Restrictions: SPO/SNE conditions cause an unpredictable result; Cannot set flags (no S variant; ACC rides the flag bit); Pattern detection stalls until any outstanding reads into CFS/CFM have returned; Conditional on BS: the BS flag is not guaranteed to match the HOLD's own detector evaluation unless the detector inputs are stable

plain

E_[COND_]HOLD ([cond])

fixed bits = decode mask/match; tinted cells = operand and modifier fields; dim 0 = reserved-zero canonical output

MEM_REG 0x1D memory 2 forms 10 mnemonics public evidence notes

Register-plus-register addressed memory access: LDR_REG reads and STR_REG writes at an address formed from Raddr and Roffset (bit 30 selects the direction; exact offset arithmetic still needs confirmation), for walking buffers whose stride lives in a register. Loads come in word, byte (B), and sign-extended byte (BS) forms; stores in word and byte. Performance matches the immediate-offset E_LDR and E_STR: the access is submitted in one cycle and DFETCH lands read data in the background.

Restrictions: No support for SPO/SNE condition flags; Reads using CFS as the pointer see the bottom 8 bits of CFS as 0x00; hardware behavior still needs final confirmation; CFS cannot be the destination of a byte read; S on byte loads means signed access (bit 21), not set-flags; no flag setting in this family; Offset semantics need confirmation: AN14650 5.26 says the offset size is one byte; the current model adds the full 32-bit offset-register value and uses the 4-bit roffset field [27:24]; Word accesses truncate the address to word alignment (bits [1:0] ignored)

  • S suffix means SIGNED (bit 21), as in the LDR family. Base register in src1 [17:14], offset register in roffset [27:24].

MODIFY_GPO_BYTE 0x1E io 1 forms 1 mnemonics public evidence notes

Atomically modifies the low byte of the general-purpose outputs by applying an AND mask, an OR mask, and an XOR mask in one instruction. Only GPO[7:0] is affected: the upper 24 bits pass through unchanged (the AND mask applies one-extended, the OR/XOR masks zero-extended).

Restrictions: No conditional variant: the AND mask byte occupies the cond field bits; Encoding remains marked for hardware confirmation

default

E_MODIFY_GPO_BYTE (andmask, ormask, xormask)

fixed bits = decode mask/match; tinted cells = operand and modifier fields; dim 0 = reserved-zero canonical output
Mnemonic Base Word Notes
E_MODIFY_GPO_BYTE 0x0000001e Applies the AND, OR, and XOR mask bytes to GPO[7:0]; the upper 24 bits pass through unchanged
  • decode mask 0x0000001f, match 0x0000001e
